# Sallow — Art Catalog

*Production guide and complete drawing checklist for the lead dev (iPad Pro + Apple Pencil). Generated 2026-10-15 from the live game code (`src/`), `ART_SPEC.md`, `ART_LIST.md` and `assets_templates/`. Where this document and `ART_SPEC.md` disagree, this document is newer (see “Things that differ from ART_SPEC.md” in section 5).*

**How to use it:** read sections 1–3 once, then work down section 4 in the order of section 7. Every PNG goes into the matching folder under `assets/` (the folder tree already exists, each folder has a `README.md` with the same specs), and it replaces the placeholder with the same name (`tools/scan-assets.ps1` or the next push makes it live). The game falls back to its generated art for anything not drawn, so you can ship piece by piece.

| Group | Entries | PNG files | Core | Important | Polish | Loader supports today | Needs engine work |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| B. Monsters | 34 | 179 | 85 | 64 | 30 | 179 | 0 |
| C. Bosses | 20 | 86 | 12 | 25 | 49 | 37 | 49 |
| D. The Fallen | 10 | 57 | 0 | 22 | 35 | 57 | 0 |

| G. Props | 40 | 55 | 26 | 5 | 24 | 35 | 20 |

| **Total** | **781** | **3026** | **345** | **557** | **2124** | **2168** | **858** |

*Entry = one checklist row (a sheet set, a family, a pack of icons). PNG file = one file you export. “Core / Important / Polish” count PNG files. Counts use the **recommended smooth frame counts** (section 2), not the minimum.*

**Workload in animation frames** (one frame = one cell with all 5 directions drawn is 5 images; the numbers below count single images, i.e. frames × 5 directions): player bodies and gear 90,210, monsters 7,910, bosses 4,460, the fallen 2,710, npcs and townsfolk 480 (R17 smooth counts).

> **Reality check.** Drawing 285 gear overlays at 62 frames × 5 directions each is ~88,000 images by hand — not feasible. Do the **core** set by hand (shared body + the 31 generic overlays) and render the family overlays through the 3D route (section 2) or leave them to the game, which recolours the generic overlays per family.

Legend — **Pri**: *core* = the game looks unfinished without it; *important* = clearly visible, next wave; *polish* = nice, the game already has a stand-in. **Loader**: *Yes* = `src/engine/assets.js` loads it today; *ENGINE En* = the file name/format is proposed here and needs the engine/UI change listed in section 5 (E-number). **Paint over** = the generated template in `assets_templates/` to open in Procreate and paint on a new layer (same size, grid, anchor).

## 1. How to deliver (iPad Pro + Apple Pencil)

### Apps

| Job | Recommended app | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Pixel-exact characters, tiles, props, icons (the crisp Diablo II look) | **Pixaki 4** (iPad, paid) — alternative **Pixquare** | Real Apple Pencil pixel drawing, onion-skin timeline, custom frame/cell grid, exports a PNG sprite sheet (set columns = frames, one row per direction) and per-frame PNGs. |
| Painterly work: UI frames, logo, title art, splash, portraits, icons you want to paint big | **Procreate** (+ Procreate Dreams for animatics) | Best brush feel; 2D grid guide at the cell size; Animation Assist for rough timing; exports transparent PNG. |
| Layered/vector UI frames, logo, anything that must stay crisp at several sizes | **Affinity Designer 2** (iPad) | Vector frames and 9-slice borders, exports slices at exact pixel sizes. |
| Rough animation / keyposes before final paint | **Procreate Animation Assist** or **FlipaClip** | Fast thumbnail passes; export frames, then paint cleanly. |
| **3D route for smooth characters** | **Nomad Sculpt** (iPad) → **Mixamo** (web, free, auto-rig + animation library) → **Blender** (on your PC) | See section 2. Blender does not run on iPad; render on the PC. |
| Assembling sheets, downscaling, batch export | **ImageMagick** on the PC (`magick montage`, `magick mogrify`) | One command lays frames into a sheet; Lanczos downscale from 3–4× art. |
| Getting files to the PC | **Files app → SMB share** / **iCloud Drive for Windows** / **Working Copy** (git client) | See “Getting files into the project”. |

### Canvas setup per asset type

Always: **RGBA, sRGB, transparent background, export PNG at 100% (never upscaled), 2 px empty margin in every cell, light from the top-left, no baked ground shadow.** In Procreate set the canvas size in *pixels* (Canvas → Crop & Resize), turn on **Drawing Guide → 2D grid** at the cell size, and hide the background colour layer before export.

| Asset | Canvas (px) | Grid / cell | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Character animation (96 cell) | frames × 96 wide, **480** tall (5 rows) | 96 × 96 | e.g. walk 16 frames = 1536 × 480. Row order top→bottom: **S, SW, W, NW, N** (S faces the viewer). Feet anchor **(48, 84)** in every frame. |
| Big creature / boss (192 cell) | frames × 192 wide, **960** tall | 192 × 192 | Anchor **(96, 168)**. A 12-frame boss sheet is 2304 × 960. Keep width ≤ 4096 (iOS texture limit): ≤ 21 frames at 192. |
| Gear overlay | identical to the base body sheet of the same animation | 96 × 96 | Same frame count as `player_base` for that animation (a different count is resampled since R17, but equal counts keep every pose aligned). |
| Tile sheet | **256 × 1344** | 64 × 96 cells, 4 columns × 14 rows | Floor diamond **64 × 32** at the bottom of each cell (y 64–95); walls rise 48 px. Draw tiles seam-free at the 2:1 angle. |
| Prop | frames × 96 wide, **128** tall | 96 × 128 | Floor-tile centre at **(48, 112)**; diamond spans x 16–79, y 96–127. |
| Icon | 64 × 64 (status icons 32 × 32) | — | Object centred, natural colours, bold shapes (shown at 16–48 px). |
| Projectile | 32 × 32 | — | Points **right**, centred (16,16). |
| FX strip | N × 128 wide, **128** tall | 128 × 128 square frames | Bright core, soft falloff, transparent edges, no black. |
| Portrait | 96 × 96 | — | Head and shoulders, facing slightly right. |
| UI 9-slice frame | as listed | corner size in the row | Corners never stretch, edges tile or stretch. Keep the centre transparent unless stated. |
| App icon | 1024 × 1024 master | — | No transparency, no rounded corners (iOS rounds them), emblem inside the middle 80%. |

**Pixel-exact or painted-then-downscaled?** Both load fine (the loader scales nothing). *Pixel-exact* (Pixaki/Pixquare at 1:1) gives the crisp D2 look and can never blur. *Painted-then-downscaled*: paint at an **integer multiple** (3× or 4×: a 96 cell becomes 288 or 384 px), then reduce with a Lanczos filter in one batch (`magick mogrify -filter Lanczos -resize 33.3333% *.png`) for softer, more painterly shading. Pick one per asset family and stay with it — mixed softness looks like two different games. Tiles: always draw at 4× (256 × 128 diamond) so the 2:1 line comes out clean after the 4:1 reduction.

### Export

1. Export **PNG** (Procreate: Share → PNG; Pixaki: Export → Sprite Sheet PNG). Not JPG, not PSD, not “transparent PNG with a white matte”.
2. Name files exactly as in the checklist (all lower case; `a-z 0-9 _` only — the loader’s pattern is `^(tiles|sprites|props|icons|projectiles|portraits|gear|fx|ui)/[a-z0-9_]+(/[a-z0-9_]+)?\.png$`).
3. Check the size in the file info (a wrong-size sheet is silently ignored and the generated art stays — that is the usual “why is my file not showing” cause).

### Getting files into the project

Pick one; all work with the repository folder `C:\Users\maxka\source\repos\sallow`.

| Route | Setup | Flow |
|---|---|---|
| **SMB share (fastest)** | On the PC share the repo’s `assets` folder (right-click → Give access to). On iPad: Files → ⋯ → Connect to Server → `smb://<pc-name>`. | In Procreate/Pixaki: Share → Save to Files → pick the `assets/<folder>`; the file lands in the repo instantly. Reload the game. |
| **iCloud Drive** | Install *iCloud for Windows*, make a folder `iCloud Drive/sallow-art/` mirroring `assets/`. | Save from the iPad to that folder; on the PC run `robocopy "%USERPROFILE%\iCloudDrive\sallow-art" "C:\Users\maxka\source\repos\sallow\assets" *.png /S` to copy. |
| **Working Copy** (git client for iPad) | Clone the repo, create a branch `art`. | Save to Files → Working Copy → `assets/…`, commit and push from the iPad; merge on the PC. Keeps history of every drawing. |
| **AirDrop** | Only to a Mac. On Windows use one of the above. | — |

**Workflow.** Every file in this checklist already exists under `assets/` as a **placeholder** with the exact path, name, canvas size, cell grid and frame count (faint cell borders, the red feet-anchor cross, frame numbers, direction labels S / SW / W / NW / N, and where the game has procedural art a rough sketch of it). **Save your finished file over the placeholder with the same name. It goes live on the next push (or run `tools/scan-assets.ps1` to test locally).** Nothing needs listing by hand: `assets/assets.js` is generated and lists only files that are not placeholders (`assets/placeholders.json` holds each placeholder's SHA-1), so the game keeps its generated art for everything you have not replaced. Optional per-file `fps` / `loop` / `stride` go in `assets/timing.json`.
Test on the iPad itself: run `serve.ps1` on the PC and open `http://<pc-ip>:<port>` in Safari (or over Tailscale), add to Home Screen, and judge the art at real size — 1 file pixel is about 1 screen pixel.

## 2. Smooth animation

**The engine plays any frame count (R17).** The frame count of a sheet is simply `width ÷ cell`; the frames are spread over the animation's duration (idle 1.0 s loop, attack 0.27 s, cast 0.33 s, hit 0.2 s, die 0.5 s), or play at the file's own rate when it is listed as `{ file, fps }`. The **walk** is different: it advances with the ground the character actually covers (one loop per ~89 px of screen ground for a 96 cell at the default timing, `{ file, stride }` to change it), so feet never slide at any speed. Trailing empty frames are ignored. The full rules, with how frames map to time, are in ART_SPEC.md → "Smooth animation". Draw the counts below; they are the recommended **smooth** set and are what the totals in this catalog use.

| Animation | Current generated | **Player (96)** | Standard monster (96) | Small creature (96) | Big / Fallen (192) | Boss (192) | NPC |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| idle | 4 | **8** | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 |
| walk | 8 | **16** | 12 | 12 | 12 | 12 | — |
| attack | 6 | **12** | 10 | 10 | 12 | 12 | — |
| cast | 6 | **10** | 10 | 10 | 10 | 10 | — |
| hit | 2 | **4** | 4 | 3 | 4 | 4 | — |
| die | 6 | **12** | 10 | 10 | 12 | 12 | — |

- **Minimum viable monster:** `idle`, `walk`, `attack`, `die` (and `hit` if you can). `cast` is only listed for casters/ranged; a missing `cast` falls back to `attack`, anything else missing falls back to `idle`.
- **All layers of one animation should have the same frame count** — base body, every gear overlay of the player, and the weapon layers. Since R17 a different count is resampled onto the body's (a 10-frame overlay on a 12-frame body repeats two frames), which keeps a half-finished set working, but only equal counts keep every pose aligned. Decide the player counts once (the table above) and never change them.
- **Feet anchor fixed.** The point between the feet stays on pixel (48, 84) (96 cell) or (96, 168) (192 cell) in *every* frame of *every* animation — including `die`, where the body falls *around* the anchor. A drifting anchor reads as foot-sliding or jitter. Put a one-pixel guide dot on a locked layer at the anchor.
- **Loops close.** Walk/idle: the frame after the last must be the first, so do not draw the contact pose twice. Idle: breathe in 8 frames, nothing moves more than 1–2 px, so 6–8 players standing together do not look synchronised (the game offsets idle phase per entity).
- **Attack has three beats:** anticipation (frames 1–3), strike with the weapon fully extended (4–6, keep the weapon visible and unsmeared), recovery (7–12). The sheet plays in 0.27 s (or at its `fps`); make the strike obvious on one frame so it still reads at that speed. When the player's swing connects the game holds the frame at the middle of the attack for a beat (hit-stop), so put the fully extended strike pose there (frames 6–7 of 12).
- **`die`:** last frame is the corpse and stays on the ground — paint blood pools into it. **`hit`:** a recoil of 1–3 px with a flash-friendly silhouette (the game adds the white flash).
- **Flyers** (bats, imps, gazers) hover ~10 px above the anchor with a bob in idle/walk; the shadow stays on the ground (the game draws it).
- **Directions:** draw 5 (S, SW, W, NW, N); the game mirrors SW/W/NW into SE/E/NE, which swaps weapon hands. For the player, bosses and the Fallen you may later add the 3 extra rows (file name `<anim>_8.png`, rows S, SW, W, NW, N, NE, E, SE); not needed for a first pass.
- **Memory.** Decoded, one sheet is `width × height × 4` bytes: a 96-cell monster set at the recommended counts is ≈ 10 MB, a boss set ≈ 43 MB, the player body ≈ 11 MB. The game must load per theme and release on leaving (E10) — do not worry about it while drawing, but do not exceed these frame counts.

### The 3D-render route for characters (Nomad Sculpt → Mixamo → Blender)

Use it for the **player body + all gear overlays, bosses and the big Fallen** — anything where 12–16 frames × 5 directions × many pieces would take months by hand. Small monsters and NPC idles are quick to draw by hand.

1. **Sculpt** the body in Nomad Sculpt (low-poly, ~15–25k tris is plenty), paint colours in Nomad (vertex/texture), export **OBJ or glTF**. Armour/weapons are separate objects, sculpted over the body.
2. **Rig/animate** in Mixamo (upload the body as OBJ/FBX, auto-rig, pick idle / walk / sword attack / cast / hit / death clips; download FBX *with skin*). Use **one skeleton for every class** so overlays fit.
3. **Blender** (PC): import, set the **render** like this:
   - Orthographic camera, rotation **X = 60°, Z = 45°** (true 2:1 isometric), transparent film, no ground, no shadow catcher.
   - Scale so one floor tile = 64 px wide: **ortho scale = cell_px ÷ 45.25** (96 cell → **2.12**, 192 cell → **4.24**). A 58 px human then stands ≈ **1.5 Blender units** tall.
   - Put the model’s feet origin on the anchor: shift the camera so the origin sits at **(48, 84)** of the 96 cell (for a 96 cell `shift_y ≈ +0.375`; verify by rendering an empty at the origin and checking pixel row 84).
   - Directions: rotate the **model** (not the camera) about Z to **0°, 45°, 90°, 135°, 180°** = S, SW, W, NW, N (0° faces the viewer).
   - Render each animation at the chosen frame count (sample the clip evenly over one loop), at 3× the cell (288 px) and reduce with Lanczos. Add a thin outline (Freestyle or a Solidify-inverted-hull) and a hand paint pass on key poses for the painted Diablo II feel.
4. **Gear overlays from the same render:** for each piece render the full figure with every *other* object set to **Holdout** (Object → Visibility → Mask → Holdout), so only that piece’s pixels, correctly occluded by the body, are in the alpha. This produces `gear/<key>/<anim>.png` exactly aligned with `player_base` — the single biggest time saver in the project. Render the back directions with the same holdout setup; the game handles layer order.
5. **Assemble** frames into sheets (rows = directions S→N, columns = frames): `magick montage fr_*.png -tile 12x5 -geometry 96x96+0+0 -background none walk.png` (name frames so that sorting gives dir-major order, e.g. `d0_f00.png`).

## 3. Colour and readability rules

From `ART_SPEC.md` (still valid) plus the current rarity ladder:

- **Dark Diablo II mood, painterly.** Floors dark and in a *narrow* value range; characters, loot and props clearly lighter or darker than the floor they stand on. Keep a clean silhouette so a monster reads against a dark floor at 56 px tall.
- **Desaturated bases** (cold stone, bruise purple, bog green, rust, bone, dried blood). Saturated colour only as accents: glowing eyes, lava, spell light, loot.
- **Light from the top-left**, form shading only. **No ground shadow and no baked lighting** — the game adds contact shadows, torch light, the player’s light and fog.
- **2 px empty margin** inside every cell; nothing touches the cell edge. Keep the anchor identical frame to frame.
- **Rarity colours are the brightest things on screen — do not use these hues elsewhere** (current values from `C.RARITY`): common `#c8c8c8`, uncommon `#4ddc6a`, rare `#5b8cff`, magic `#b65bff`, legendary elite `#ffe45b`, mythic `#ff8a2b`, unique `#ff3b3b`, rift `#ff6aa8`. (`ART_SPEC.md` still lists the pre-R7.1 hues.)
- **Icons**: natural item colours, not rarity colours (the rarity comes from the frame/label). Bold shapes: the same icon is shown at 16–48 px.
- **Generic gear overlays are painted in neutral greys** (value only, little colour): the game recolours them with the family palette using a colour blend, so grey keeps full control of the shading. Family-specific overlays use the family palette listed in each row.
- **Water and lava frames** are animation frames (4 columns). Water floor stays visible through it; deep water and lava are opaque and must read as “not walkable”.
- **Smoothing is fine.** Painterly shading, soft edges and anti-aliasing are welcome, but keep the outline of characters 1 px darker than the body for separation.

## 4. The checklist

Column key: **Files / folder** is relative to `assets/`; `{a,b}` means one file per name, `<id>` is the id in the row. **Canvas** is the size of *each* file (for animation sets the widths are listed in the order the animations are named: for characters `idle / walk / attack / cast / hit / die`, only the ones present). **Frames** are frames per animation (I = idle, W = walk, A = attack, C = cast, H = hit, D = die) × directions. **Anchor** is the floor point inside the cell.

Tick a row by adding `[x]` in your own copy; the “Loader” column tells you whether you see it in the game as soon as the file replaces its placeholder (`tools/scan-assets.ps1` regenerates `assets/assets.js`).

### A. Player bodies and gear layers

Everything the hero wears is **layered**: one shared (or per-class) **base body** plus one overlay per equipped piece. Draw order is composed by the game: front rows S, SW, W (and mirrored): base → boots → chest → gloves → helm → off-hand → weapon; back rows NW, N: off-hand → weapon → base → boots → chest → gloves → helm (held items go *behind* the body). An overlay that is missing for an animation is simply not drawn, so paint **all six animations of a piece together**. Armour overlay keys are `<family>_<slot>`; weapon keys `<family>_<weaponType>` (sword, axe, mace, dagger, bow, staff, wand); off-hand keys `<family>_<kind>` (shield, orb, tome, quiver). Two-handed weapons (greatsword, greataxe, maul) and the belt/ring/amulet slots reuse the sword/axe/mace overlays or are not drawn on the body (E10). **If a hand-drawn class sheet exists but there is no base body, gear is not shown at all** — so draw the base before anything else here. Row A6 (class full sheets) is a fallback only.

### B. Monsters

One folder per monster: `sprites/<id>/<anim>.png`. The weapon is painted into the sprite. Casters and ranged monsters get a `cast` sheet (their shot/spell); the rest fall back to `attack`. Palette hexes are the colours the procedural sprite uses today — a good starting point, not a rule. `B-minion`: bone-raised helpers the necromancer summons (they should read as *friendly*: a faint cold-blue edge is applied by the game).

*34 entries · 179 files · 7,910 images to paint · core 85 / important 64 / polish 30*

### C. Bosses

Bosses use the 192 cell. Each boss has three phases with different mechanics; today **one sprite set serves all phases** (the game adds a rim and a few effects). Phase variants (C2) are an optional upgrade that needs E7. Boss-owned objects (C3) are the stationary things a fight spawns.

*20 entries · 86 files · 4,460 images to paint · core 12 / important 25 / polish 49*

### D. The Fallen (ten dead adventurers; each a mini-boss)

Ten dead adventurers who fought their way down and became undead mini-bosses (`adv_fallen_N`; the relic they drop has the matching `fallen_N` id, see icons H9). They are **violet-tinged** by the game, and they are *characters*: give each a distinct silhouette and signature piece of kit. The class sheet of their class is the fallback.

*10 entries · 57 files · 2,710 images to paint · core 0 / important 22 / polish 35*

### F. Tiles

Five theme sheets, 23 optional floor-variant sheets. Every theme sheet fills all 14 rows (the generated templates do), so a theme sheet is **37 cells**. Variant sheets are used *untinted*; without one the game tints the theme sheet to the variant palette automatically, so they are polish. Paint over `assets_templates/tiles/<theme>.png`.

*28 entries · 28 files · 1,036 images to paint · core 2 / important 3 / polish 23*

#### F1 Theme sheets (every theme uses all 14 rows — see the row table)

**Row table (every theme sheet; rows from top, columns left → right):**

| Row | Tile | Columns used | Notes |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 0 | Floor | 4: plain (56%), cracked (12%), stained (19%), debris (12%) | make column 0 the plainest; flat diamond only, the 64 px above must stay transparent |
| 1 | Wall | 4 variants | tall: top face y 16–47, left/right faces 32 × 48; right face darker; the game cuts walls down when they hide the player, so the top face must read alone |
| 2 | Door | 2: col 0 wall running top-left → bottom-right, col 1 the other axis (empty = mirrored) | open doorway frame, floor visible inside |
| 3 | Stairs down | 1 | must read instantly as a way down |
| 4 | Stairs up | 1 | may use the whole 96 px cell height |
| 5 | Shallow water | 4 animation frames (~3 fps) | floor visible through it |
| 6 | Grass | 3 variants | dead, yellowed (town mostly) |
| 7 | Path | 2 variants | mud, ash, cobbles |
| 8 | Shallow lava | 4 animation frames (~2 fps) | glowing cracks over dark crust |
| 9 | Bridge | 1 | planks/slab over water, lava or a pit |
| 10 | Rubble | 2 variants | walkable debris |
| 11 | Deep water | 4 animation frames | dark, opaque, clearly not walkable |
| 12 | Deep lava | 4 animation frames | bright, churning |
| 13 | Chasm | 1 | near-black pit with a faint rim |

### G. Props

Props are 96 × 128 cells; the tile the prop stands on has its centre at (48, 112). Frames: 3+ frames loop at 8 fps, 2 frames alternate slowly (~2.5 fps) — for slow pulses keep 2 until per-file fps exists (E2). `gate` is not an animation: frame 1 is one door axis, frame 2 the other.

*40 entries · 55 files · 120 images to paint · core 26 / important 5 / polish 24*

### H. Items and icons

**Important limitation (E1):** `icons/<shape>.png` replaces *every* item/skill that uses that shape, and the file is drawn **untinted**. So drawing `gem.png` makes all 30 gems and 15 bloodstones look identical, `rune.png` all 8 materials, `sword.png` all 8 tiers of sword. Draw H1 (the 40 shapes) only as the **fallback**; the real work is H2–H9, which need the engine to look up `icons/items/<baseId>`, `icons/uniques/<id>`, `icons/gems/…` first. Ask for E1 before spending time on gems and materials.

*180 entries · 410 files · 410 images to paint · core 42 / important 254 / polish 114*

### I. Skill and perk icons

Skills currently show a *shape* icon in a colour. Per-skill painted icons need E1. Perks have no icon at all today. Paint a glyph on a transparent background — the game draws the slot ring/frame around it.

*30 entries · 216 files · 216 images to paint · core 0 / important 80 / polish 136*

### J. Projectiles and VFX

Projectiles are single images that rotate. FX strips are played once over the effect’s length and scaled to its radius, and **are not recoloured**: one strip serves every damage type until the per-element variants (J4, E3) exist. Without a file the game keeps its particle effects, which already look decent.

*44 entries · 64 files · 702 images to paint · core 6 / important 14 / polish 44*

### K. UI art (everything below is built from CSS in src/ui/style.js today; image skins need ENGINE E5 — a per-element image hook)

The whole UI is DOM/CSS today with hard-edged gradients (no images), so **every UI file needs a hook (E5)** except the joystick pair. Frames are 9-slice (CSS `border-image`) so one small file skins any size. Sizes below assume the 375-pt-wide phone layout at 2× density.

*63 entries · 144 files · 144 images to paint · core 18 / important 54 / polish 72*

## 5. For the engine team

Everything below is a request *from the art catalog*; nothing here is implemented. File naming is a proposal — change it if the code prefers another shape, but then update the READMEs.

| # | Request | Why art needs it | Rows affected |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Keyed icon lookup.** Look up `icons/items/<baseId>`, `icons/uniques/<itemId>`, `icons/gems/<kind>_<tier>`, `icons/blood/<kind>_<tier>`, `icons/materials/<matKey>`, `icons/consumables/<id>`, `icons/relics/<id>`, `icons/skills/<skillId>`, `icons/perks/<perkId>`, `icons/class/<classId>` first, then fall back to `icons/<shape>`. The icon descriptor (`{shape,color,accent}`) carries no id today, so the item/skill/perk id has to be threaded to `getIcon`. Perks need an icon slot in the UI. Optionally tint-by-descriptor for hand-drawn shapes. | Today a hand-drawn `icons/<shape>.png` is drawn untinted for *every* thing with that shape: all gems, all bloodstones, all 8 materials and every tier of every weapon would look the same. | H2–H9, I1–I4 |
| E2 | **Animation timing and layers.** (a) Per-file `fps` in `assets/assets.js` — **done (R17)**, plus `stride` for walks. (b) Resample gear overlays to the base’s frame count — **done** (composed sheets also keep the body’s fps / stride since R17). (c) Tile animation: let water/lava use more than 4 columns (e.g. `tiles/<theme>_anim.png`) and allow per-tile fps. (d) Props: allow 2-frame props to play at a chosen rate; a 4-warmth strip for `rift_shard`. (e) Optional `hit` point/“strike frame” marker per attack file. (f) Walk cycle tied to ground speed — **done (R17)**. | Smooth counts (12-frame walks, 10-frame attacks) mean every layer of an animation must agree; slow pulses with many frames would run at 8 fps. | A, G, F |
| E3 | **FX and projectile formats.** (a) Per-rarity loot beams `fx/lootbeam_<rarity>.png`. (b) Per-element variants `fx/<type>_<element>` chosen by the effect’s `dtype`/colour. (c) New strip names: `flow`, `closecall`, `ember`, `heat`, `wisp`, `bargain`, `shrine`, `telegraph`, `pool_poison`, `pool_fire`, `grave`, `spawn`, `death_dust`, `rift_pulse`. (d) Optional animated projectiles (frames in one strip) and non-square strips. (e) Strips are not recoloured: allow an optional tint flag so one white strip can serve many colours. | Hand-drawn strips override the colourful procedural bursts with a single look. | J2–J5 |
| E4 | **Ground art.** Gold amount tiers (`icons/gold_small/medium/large`), a loot-pile heap sprite (`icons/pile.png`), and corpse/blood decals. | Loot is the game; piles are drawn procedurally today. | H8 |
| E5 | **UI skinning hook.** Let `src/ui/style.js` take `url(assets/ui/…)` for frames (`border-image`, 9-slice), buttons, rarity borders, orbs, bars, skill/perk nodes, markers; draw the world markers (target ring, stair arrows, quest `!`/`?`) from `ui/markers/*`; map icons from `ui/map/icons.png`; wire `ui/logo.png`, `ui/title_bg_*`, `ui/appicon/*` (+ `<link rel=apple-touch-startup-image>` set), a **buff/status row** using `icons/status/*`. Each file must be optional (CSS fallback). | The entire UI is CSS gradients with hard stops today; there are no image slots except the joystick pair. | K, I5 |
| E6 | **Theme prop variants** `props/<type>_<theme>.png`; **two-handed overlays** (`<family>_greatsword`, `_greataxe`, `_maul`) instead of reusing sword/axe/mace; optional belt/ring/amulet visuals on the body (none are drawn now). | Dungeon decor is dressed per theme (DECOR in `world/populate.js`) but every prop has one look; two-handed weapons look one-handed. | G3, A4 |
| E7 | **Boss phase variants and owned objects.** Load `<anim>_p2`, `_p3` for a boss by phase; give `boss_prop` objects their own sprite id (egg sac, rope anchor, …) instead of one blob with a colour override. | Phase changes are a big part of the boss fantasy. | C2, C3 |
| E8 | **Portraits for monsters.** `getPortrait` already accepts `portraits/<monsterId>.png`; the UI would have to ask for them in the boss intro, Fallen banner and relic cards. | Optional polish; the loader is ready. | L3 |
| E9 | **NPCs.** An innkeeper role (`npc_innkeeper`), several refugee looks (`npc_refugee_1..3`), sheets for named quest-givers if they ever appear in the world. | The inn exists as props only; refugees currently share one sprite. | E |
| E10 | **Loading and memory.** (a) `assets.js` loads at most **3000** files from `assets/assets.js` (`i < 3000`); this catalog is ~3000 files — raise the cap or load a per-theme manifest lazily. (b) Load per theme/act and release on leaving (a boss set at recommended counts is ≈ 40 MB decoded, 6 bosses + Fallen would not fit together on an iPhone). (c) `sw.js` precaches every listed asset at install — switch to runtime caching or per-act packs before the art lands. (d) Consider an atlas build step (pack per theme into one PNG + JSON). | Without it, hand-drawn art will crash or stall Safari on phones. | all |
| E11 | **Echo and class sheets.** Decide what draws the Echo (its own `sprites/echo` sheet or `player_base` + its recorded equipment, ghost-tinted) and whether `sprites/<class>/` full sheets are kept only as fallbacks. | Avoids drawing a sheet that is never shown. | A6, B-echo |
| E12 | **Tool/doc upkeep.** `tests/assets.html` (generator of `ART_LIST.md`) does not list `props/inn`, `props/inn_fire` (both exist), and ART_SPEC.md still has pre-R7.1 rarity hexes and the old 8/6/6/2/6 frame table. Re-generate/update after the format decisions in this document. | Keeps the lists honest. | — |

**Things that differ from `ART_SPEC.md`** (this document wins): rarity hexes (R7.1 ladder above); recommended frame counts (any count is accepted; ART_SPEC → "Smooth animation" carries the same table since R17); props `inn` and `inn_fire`; the extra item/skill/perk/UI paths marked ENGINE; the player is layered so the hand-drawn class sheets are a fallback.

## 7. Suggested drawing order (biggest visual difference first)

| Step | What | Files | Images |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | Crypt tileset (`tiles/crypt.png`) and the crypt props: chest, chest_open, barrel, crate, torch, shrine, pillar, bones, tombstone, gate, corpse — the floor fills the whole screen, so this changes the look of the game more than anything else | 16 | 76 |
| 2 | Player: `player_base` (idle, walk, attack, die first) + the 31 generic overlays — the hero is on screen every second | 138 | 7,130 |
| 3 | Crypt monsters + Morgrath (`boss_gravelord`) + the minions | 55 | 1,986 |
| 4 | The 40 shape icons (H1) + HUD essentials: orb frame/glass/fill, bar frame, skill slot, attack/dash buttons, panel + dialog + tooltip frames, inventory cell, rarity frames (needs E5) | 96 | 96 |
| 5 | Town: `tiles/town.png`, townsfolk idles, town props (stall, well, fountain, sign, tree, bush, campfire, dungeon_entrance, chute, surface_crate, inn, inn_fire, capped, rift_shard), core portraits | 34 | 443 |
| 6 | Effects that sell combat and loot: `fx/hit`, `slash`, `explosion`, `nova`, `levelup`, `lootbeam`, the six projectiles, per-rarity loot beams (E3) | 20 | 174 |
| 7 | Caves: tileset, monsters, Skarn (`boss_broodmother`), the first four Fallen | 71 | 2,257 |
| 8 | Item icons that tell tiers apart: weapon and chest families (H2 important), gems and bloodstones (H4, H5), materials, consumables (needs E1) | 184 | 184 |
| 9 | Skill icons by class (I1, I2), uniques with their own icon (H3 legendary/mythic/rift), set items | 150 | 150 |
| 10 | Inferno: tileset, monsters, Azgalor; Abyss: tileset, monsters, Xal’thuun, The Choir, Oswin Marrow | 94 | 3,373 |
| 11 | Rift looks, remaining Fallen, remaining NPCs, the remaining important UI, title logo and background, app icon and splash set | 133 | 1,761 |
| 12 | Family gear overlays (A3–A5) — by the 3D holdout route; weapons families last | 1578 | 81,530 |
| 13 | Everything else: floor-variant tilesets (F2), perk icons, status icons, boss phase variants (E7), theme prop variants (E6), per-element FX (J4), remaining portraits | 457 | 6,677 |

After step 3 the first dungeon is fully hand-drawn; after step 7 act I (depths 1–10) looks finished; step 10 completes the story acts. Steps 11–13 are where the 3D route pays for itself.
